b_{c}(z)=b_{c}(z^{\prime})=0
, then for every
q\in\mathbf{Z}
,

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})z_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}\right|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})z_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})z_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})z_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}|}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|z-z^{\prime}\right|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}.

Proof.

By definition
c=[c_{k}]
for some sequence
(c_{k})\in\Pi_{\omega}\mathcal{C}_{k}
where
\varphi_{k}(g)
fixes
c_{k}
,
\omega
-as.
Without loss of generality, we can assume that
x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathring{B}(c_{k},\rho_{k})

\omega
-as.
For every
k\in\mathbf{N}
, we denote by

\theta_{k}\colon\mathring{B}(c_{k},\rho_{k})\times\mathring{B}(c_{k},\rho_{k})\to\mathbf{R}/\Theta_{k}\mathbf{Z}

the angle cocycle provided by Axiom
(H5)
.
Since
\theta_{k}
is a
\mathchoice{\operatorname{Stab}\left(c_{k}\right)}{\operatorname{Stab}(c_{k})}{\operatorname{Stab}(c_{k})}{\operatorname{Stab}(c_{k})}
-invariant cocycle, the quantity
\theta_{k}(\varphi_{k}(g)x_{k},x_{k})
does not depend on the point
x_{k}
.
For simplicity we write

\alpha_{k}=\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}(g)x_{k},x_{k}\right).

for the unique representative of
\theta_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}(g)x_{k},x_{k}\right)
in
(-\Theta_{k}/2,\Theta_{k}/2]
.
By assumption
\eta(g)\neq 1
, hence
\varphi_{k}(g)
is non-trivial
\omega
-as.
Consequently
\alpha_{k}\neq 0

\omega
-as.
On the other hand,
\eta(g)
fixes pointwise
\bar{P}(c)
.
It follows from
Lemma 7.3
that
(\alpha_{k})
converges to zero.
More precisely we have

\ln\mathchoice{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}{|\alpha_{k}|}{|\alpha_{k}|}{|\alpha_{k}|}\leqslant-\frac{\rho_{k}}{\varepsilon_{k}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{k}}\right).

Hence there exists a sequence of integers
(m_{k})
such that

\mathchoice{\left|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}\left(g^{nm_{k}}\right)x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\right)\right|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}\left(g^{nm_{k}}\right)x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\right)|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}\left(g^{nm_{k}}\right)x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\right)|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}\left(g^{nm_{k}}\right)x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\right)|}\leqslant\frac{n}{2}\mathchoice{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}{|\alpha_{k}|}{|\alpha_{k}|}{|\alpha_{k}|},\ \text{ $\omega$-as}.

(19)

We let
g_{k}=g^{m_{k}}
, for every
k\in\mathbf{N}
.
Let us now check the properties of
(g_{k})
.
Since
\eta(g)
pointwise fixes
\bar{P}(c)
so does
\eta(g_{k})
, hence Point
(C2)((A3))i
holds.
It follows from our choice of
(m_{k})
that

\ln\mathchoice{\left|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}\left(g_{k}^{n}\right)x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\right)\right|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}\left(g_{k}^{n}\right)x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\right)|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}\left(g_{k}^{n}\right)x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\right)|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}\left(g_{k}^{n}\right)x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\right)|}\leqslant\ln\mathchoice{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}{|\alpha_{k}|}{|\alpha_{k}|}{|\alpha_{k}|}+\ln\left(\frac{n}{2}\right)\leqslant-\frac{\rho_{k}}{\varepsilon_{k}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{k}}\right).

Point
(C2)((A3))ii
now follows from
Lemma 7.3
.
For the rest of the proof we will assume that
m_{k}\neq 0

\omega
-as.
Indeed otherwise the last two points are straightforward.

Consider now a point
y=\lim_{\omega}y_{k}
and let
b_{c}(y)=-s
.
If
s=\rho
, then
\rho
is finite and
y
coincide with
c=\lim_{\omega}c_{k}
.
In this case Point
(C2)((A3))iii
follows from the fact that
g_{k}
fixes
c_{k}
.
Suppose now that
s<\rho
.
Without loss of generality we can assume that
y_{k}\in\mathring{B}(c_{k},\rho_{k})

\omega
-as.
It follows from our assumption that
\mathchoice{\left|x-x^{\prime}\right|}{|x-x^{\prime}|}{|x-x^{\prime}|}{|x-x^{\prime}|}\leqslant 2s<2\rho
.
Hence
Lemma 7.3
tells us that
\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\right)
converges to zero and

\ln\mathchoice{\left|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\right)\right|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\right)|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\right)|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\right)|}\leqslant-\frac{\rho_{k}}{\varepsilon_{k}}+\frac{\mathchoice{\left|x-x^{\prime}\right|}{|x-x^{\prime}|}{|x-x^{\prime}|}{|x-x^{\prime}|}}{2\varepsilon_{k}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{k}}\right).

Note that

n\,\theta_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}(g_{k})y_{k},y_{k}\right)=\theta_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}(g^{nm_{k}})x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\right)+\theta_{k}\left(x^{\prime}_{k},x_{k}\right)

It follows from our choice of
(m_{k})
that representatives in
(-\Theta_{k}/2,\Theta_{k}/2]
of both terms on the right hand side of the previous equation converge to zero.
Consequently the representative of
\theta_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}(g_{k})y_{k},y_{k}\right)
in the same interval also converges to zero and satisfies

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}(g_{k})y_{k},y_{k}\right)\right|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}(g_{k})y_{k},y_{k}\right)|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}(g_{k})y_{k},y_{k}\right)|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}(g_{k})y_{k},y_{k}\right)|}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{n}\mathchoice{\left|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}(g^{nm_{k}})x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\right)\right|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}(g^{nm_{k}})x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\right)|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}(g^{nm_{k}})x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\right)|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}(g^{nm_{k}})x_{k},x^{\prime}_{k}\right)|}+\frac{1}{n}\mathchoice{\left|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(x^{\prime}_{k},x_{k}\right)\right|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(x^{\prime}_{k},x_{k}\right)|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(x^{\prime}_{k},x_{k}\right)|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(x^{\prime}_{k},x_{k}\right)|}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\mathchoice{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}{|\alpha_{k}|}{|\alpha_{k}|}{|\alpha_{k}|}+\frac{1}{n}\mathchoice{\left|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(x^{\prime}_{k},x_{k}\right)\right|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(x^{\prime}_{k},x_{k}\right)|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(x^{\prime}_{k},x_{k}\right)|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(x^{\prime}_{k},x_{k}\right)|}.

Recall that if
a,b\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
, then
\ln(a+b)\leqslant\max\{\ln a,\ln b\}+\ln 2
.
It follows from our previous observations that for every
q\in\mathbf{Z}
,

\ln\mathchoice{\left|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k},y_{k}\right)\right|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k},y_{k}\right)|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k},y_{k}\right)|}{|\tilde{\theta}_{k}\left(\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k},y_{k}\right)|}\leqslant-\frac{\rho_{k}}{\varepsilon_{k}}+\frac{\mathchoice{\left|x-x^{\prime}\right|}{|x-x^{\prime}|}{|x-x^{\prime}|}{|x-x^{\prime}|}}{2\varepsilon_{k}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{k}}\right)\leqslant-\frac{\rho_{k}-s}{\varepsilon_{k}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{k}}\right)

Using again
Lemma 7.3
we get that
\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}-y_{k}\right|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}-y_{k}|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}-y_{k}|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}-y_{k}|}=0
.

Let
z=\lim_{\omega}z_{k}
and
z^{\prime}=\lim_{\omega}z_{k}
in
X_{\omega}
such that
\mathchoice{\left|z-z^{\prime}\right|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}\geqslant\mathchoice{\left|x-x^{\prime}\right|}{|x-x^{\prime}|}{|x-x^{\prime}|}{|x-x^{\prime}|}
and
b_{c}(z)=b_{c}(z^{\prime})=0
.
Let
q\in\mathbf{Z}
.
We denote by
y=\lim_{\omega}y_{k}
the midpoint of
\mathchoice{\left[z,z^{\prime}\right]}{[z,z^{\prime}]}{[z,z^{\prime}]}{[z,z^{\prime}]}
.
Note that

2b_{c}(y)\leqslant-\mathchoice{\left|z-z^{\prime}\right|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}\leqslant-\mathchoice{\left|x-x^{\prime}\right|}{|x-x^{\prime}|}{|x-x^{\prime}|}{|x-x^{\prime}|}.

It follows from the previous point that
\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}-y_{k}\right|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}-y_{k}|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}-y_{k}|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}-y_{k}|}=0
.
Applying the triangle inequality we get

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})z_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}\right|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})z_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})z_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})z_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}|}

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})z_{k}-\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}\right|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})z_{k}-\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})z_{k}-\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})z_{k}-\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}|}+\mathchoice{\left|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}-y_{k}\right|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}-y_{k}|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}-y_{k}|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}-y_{k}|}+\mathchoice{\left|y_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}\right|}{|y_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}|}{|y_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}|}{|y_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}|}

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|z_{k}-y_{k}\right|}{|z_{k}-y_{k}|}{|z_{k}-y_{k}|}{|z_{k}-y_{k}|}+\mathchoice{\left|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}-y_{k}\right|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}-y_{k}|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}-y_{k}|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})y_{k}-y_{k}|}+\mathchoice{\left|y_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}\right|}{|y_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}|}{|y_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}|}{|y_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}|}.

Passing to the limit we get
\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})z_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}\right|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})z_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})z_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}|}{|\varphi_{k}(g^{q}_{k})z_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k}|}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|z-y\right|}{|z-y|}{|z-y|}{|z-y|}+\mathchoice{\left|y-z^{\prime}\right|}{|y-z^{\prime}|}{|y-z^{\prime}|}{|y-z^{\prime}|}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|z-z^{\prime}\right|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}
, which completes the proof of Point
(C2)((A3))iv
.
∎

For the remainder of this section, we assume that
\rho>0
.
We also fix a cone point
c\in\mathcal{C}
and denote by
v_{c}
the vertex of
S_{\Lambda}
corresponding to the peripheral component
\bar{P}(c)
.

Notations 7.1
.

We write
N_{c}
for the pointwise stabilizer of
\bar{P}(c)
.
It is also the stabilizer of every edge of

adjacent to
v_{c}
.
In addition, we denote by

the peripheral subgroup of

(see

).
Concretely it is the pre-image of the torsion part of the finitely generated abelian group

.

Normal forms.

We now define a suitable normal form for the elements of
L
( respectively
\hat{L}_{k}
) which reflects their excursions in the peripheral subtree
\bar{P}(c)
.
Let
\bar{S}
be the tree obtained from

by collapsing every edge which is not adjacent to a vertex in the

-orbit of

.
In particular,

.
We write
\bar{v}^{+}
and

for the respective images of

and

in
\bar{S}
.
Similarly we define
\bar{S}_{k}
as the tree obtained from
\hat{S}_{k}
by collapsing every edge whose image in
S
is collapsed by the map
S\to S_{\Lambda}\to\bar{S}
.
Note that
\hat{S}_{k}\to S
yields an equivariant map
\bar{S}_{k}\to\bar{S}
such that the induced map
\bar{S}_{k}/\hat{L}_{k}\to\bar{S}/L
is an isomorphism.

We now proceed as in
Section 5.1
.
We choose a lift
D_{0}
of a maximal subtree in
\bar{S}/L
which contains
\bar{v}^{+}
.
Up to replacing

by a translate, we may assume that
\bar{v}_{c}
belongs to
D_{0}
.
We now fix a subtree
D_{1}
of
\bar{S}
such that

is contained in

and the

-orbit of every edge in
\bar{S}
contains exactly one element is
D_{1}
.
We write
V
( respectively
E
) for the vertex set of
D_{0}
( respectively the edge set of
D_{1}
).
We write
\mathbf{F}(E)
for the free group

\mathbf{F}(E)=\mathchoice{\left\langle E\mid e\bar{e}=1,\ \forall e\in E\right\rangle}{\langle E\mid e\bar{e}=1,\ \forall e\in E\rangle}{\langle E\mid e\bar{e}=1,\ \forall e\in E\rangle}{\langle E\mid e\bar{e}=1,\ \forall e\in E\rangle}

and let

\mathbf{L}=\mathbf{F}(E)\freep\left(\freep_{v\in V}L_{v}\right).

Recall that for every
k\in\mathbf{N}
, the quotient
\bar{S}/L
and
\bar{S}_{k}/\hat{L}_{k}
are isomorphic.
Hence we can choose for each
k\in\mathbf{N}
two subtrees
D_{k,0}
and
D_{k,1}
such that for every
k^{\prime}>k
, for every

, the map
\bar{S}_{k}\to\bar{S}_{k^{\prime}}
( respectively
\bar{S}_{k}\to\bar{S}
) induces an isomorphism from

to
D_{k^{\prime},i}
( respectively
D_{k,i}
to
D_{i}
).
We write
V_{k}
( respectively
E_{k}
) for the vertex set of
D_{k,0}
( respectively the edge set of
D_{k,1}
) and let

\mathbf{L}_{k}=\mathbf{F}(E_{k})\freep\left(\freep_{v\in V_{k}}L_{k,v}\right),

where
\mathbf{F}(E_{k})
is defined in the same way as
\mathbf{F}(E)
.
Observe that the maps
(\hat{L}_{k},\bar{S}_{k})\to(\hat{L}_{k^{\prime}},\bar{S}_{k^{\prime}})
and
(\hat{L}_{k},\bar{S}_{k})\to(L,\bar{S})
induce morphisms
\mathbf{L}_{k}\to\mathbf{L}_{k^{\prime}}
and
\mathbf{L}_{k}\to\mathbf{L}
.
For every
\mathbf{e}\in E_{0}
we fix an element
s(\mathbf{e})\in L_{0}
such that
s(\mathbf{e})t(\mathbf{e})
belongs to
V_{0}
.
Such an element always exists as
D_{0,0}\subset\bar{S}_{0}
is a lift of a maximal subtree of
\bar{S}_{0}/L_{0}
.x
If
\mathbf{e}
is an edge in
E_{k}
( respectively
E
), we now denote by
s(\mathbf{e})
the image in
\hat{L}_{k}
( respectively
L
) of
s(\mathbf{e}_{0})
where
\mathbf{e}_{0}
is the (unique) pre-image of
\mathbf{e}
in
E_{0}
.
With these data we can define a map
\sigma_{k}\colon\mathbf{L}_{k}\to\hat{L}_{k}
which extends the embedding of the vertex groups
\hat{L}_{k,v}\hookrightarrow\hat{L}_{k}
, for every
v\in V_{k}
, and such that
\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{e})=s(\bar{\mathbf{e}})s(\mathbf{e})^{-1}
, for every
\mathbf{e}\in E_{k}
.
We define
\sigma\colon\mathbf{L}\to L
in the exact same way so that for all integers
k^{\prime}>k
, the following diagram commutes.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{L}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{L}_{k^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{L}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{L}_{k^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\sigma_{k}
\sigma_{k^{\prime}}
\sigma

As in
Section 5.1
, these maps
\sigma_{k}
and
\sigma
provide a normal form at
\bar{v}_{+}
to write the elements of
L_{k}
and
L
respectively.

Turn at
c
.

Given
g\in L
, the next step is to analyze the non-degenerate excursions of
\mathchoice{\left[o,go\right]}{[o,go]}{[o,go]}{[o,go]}
in the peripheral subtree
\bar{P}(c)
and its translates.
To read this information on the normal form of
g
, we define a notion of turn.

Definition 7.53
.

A
turn at
c
is a triple
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})
where
\mathbf{g}\in L_{c}
while
\mathbf{e}
and
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}
are edges in
E
such that
t(\mathbf{e})
and
\iota(\mathbf{e}^{\prime})
are in the
L
-orbit of
\bar{v}_{c}
.
Such a turn is
reduced
if the decorated path
\mathbf{e}\mathbf{g}\mathbf{e}^{\prime}
is reduced.

In practice, we sometimes blur the distinction between a turn
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})
and the corresponding word
\mathbf{e}\mathbf{g}\mathbf{e}^{\prime}
of
\mathbf{L}
.
Each edge
e
in
\bar{S}
has unique pre-image in
S_{\Lambda}
.
For simplicity we still denote by
p_{e}
the corresponding attaching point of the graph of actions
\Lambda
.

Definition 7.54
.

The
realization
of a turn
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})
is the pair of points

\left(s(\mathbf{e})p_{\mathbf{e}},\mathbf{g}s(\bar{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime})p_{\bar{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime}}\right).

Remark
.

Recall that
s(\mathbf{e})
maps the endpoint
t(\mathbf{e})
to a vertex in
V
, here
\bar{v}_{c}
.
Same with
s(\bar{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime})
.
It follows that the realization
(z,z^{\prime})
of
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})
is an element of
b_{c}^{-1}(0)\times b_{c}^{-1}(0)
.
In particular,
\mathchoice{\left|z-z^{\prime}\right|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}\leqslant 2\rho
.

Lemma 7.55
.

Let
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})
be a turn at
c
and
(z,z^{\prime})
its realization.
The following are equivalent

i.

(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})
is not reduced;

ii.

\mathbf{e}^{\prime}=\bar{\mathbf{e}}
and
\mathbf{g}
belongs to
N_{c}
;

iii.

z=z^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By construction
s(\mathbf{e})\mathbf{e}
is an edge of
\bar{S}
adjacent to
\bar{v}_{c}
.
Hence its stabilizer is
N_{c}
.
In particular,
N_{c}
fixes
s(\mathbf{e})p_{\mathbf{e}}
.
The implications
(C2)((A3))i

\Rightarrow

(C2)((A3))ii
and
(C2)((A3))ii

\Rightarrow

(C2)((A3))iii
follow from these observation.
Assume now that
z=z^{\prime}
.
We built
\bar{S}
by collapsing certain edges of
S_{\Lambda}
.
Alternatively it can be obtained from
S(0)
by collapsing every edge which is not adjacent to a vertex in the orbit of the peripheral component
T\cap\bar{P}(c)
.
We make an abuse of notation and still denote by
\mathbf{e}
and
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}
their (unique) pre-images in
S(0)
.
Let
v
( respectively
v^{\prime}
) be the start ( respectively end) of
\mathbf{e}
( respectively
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}
) in
S(0)
.
Note that
v
and
v^{\prime}
are transverse vertices.
The attaching point
z=s(\mathbf{e})p_{\mathbf{e}}
and
z^{\prime}=\mathbf{g}s(\bar{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime})p_{\bar{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime}}
belongs to the transverse subtrees
s(\mathbf{e})Y_{v}
and
\mathbf{g}s(\bar{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime})Y_{v^{\prime}}
of the graph of actions
\Lambda(0)
.
Since
z=z^{\prime}
, the subtrees
s(\mathbf{e})Y_{v}
and
\mathbf{g}s(\bar{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime})Y_{v^{\prime}}
are equal, i.e.
s(\mathbf{e})v=\mathbf{g}s(\bar{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime})v^{\prime}
.
By definition of a turn,
\mathbf{g}
belongs to
L_{c}
.
Consequently
s(\mathbf{e})\mathbf{e}=\mathbf{g}s(\bar{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime})\bar{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime}
.
It follows that
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}=\bar{\mathbf{e}}
and
\mathbf{g}
fixes
s(\mathbf{e})\mathbf{e}
.
In other words
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})
is not reduced, which proves
(C2)((A3))iii

\Rightarrow

(C2)((A3))i
.
∎

We now define a finite set of turns
\mathscr{T}
as follows.
First, we choose for each generator
u\in U
a preferred normal form
\mathbf{u}
based at
\bar{v}^{+}
of
\eta(u)
:

\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{g}_{0}\mathbf{e}_{0}\mathbf{g}_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1}\dots\mathbf{g}_{m-1}\mathbf{e}_{m}\mathbf{g}_{m}

We write
\mathbf{U}
for the set of normal forms obtained in this way.
Let
\mathscr{T}
be the set of all turns
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})
at
c
such that
\mathbf{e}\mathbf{g}\mathbf{e}^{\prime}
is a subword of some
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{U}
.
We denote by
F
the set of all realizations of the turns in
\mathscr{T}
and let

F_{0}=\mathchoice{\left\{(y,y^{\prime})\in F\;:\ \mathchoice{\left|y-y^{\prime}\right|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|z-z^{\prime}\right|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|},\ \forall(z,z^{\prime})\in F\right\}}{\{(y,y^{\prime})\in F\;:\ \mathchoice{\left|y-y^{\prime}\right|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|z-z^{\prime}\right|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|},\ \forall(z,z^{\prime})\in F\}}{\{(y,y^{\prime})\in F\;:\ \mathchoice{\left|y-y^{\prime}\right|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|z-z^{\prime}\right|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|},\ \forall(z,z^{\prime})\in F\}}{\{(y,y^{\prime})\in F\;:\ \mathchoice{\left|y-y^{\prime}\right|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|z-z^{\prime}\right|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|},\ \forall(z,z^{\prime})\in F\}}

which is the set of all “shortest” realizations.

Definition 7.56
.

A turn
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{T}
is
minimal
if its realization belongs to
F_{0}
.
We denote by
\mathscr{T}_{0}
the set of all minimal turns.

Recall that
N_{c}
stands for the pointwise stabilizer of
\bar{P}(c)
while
K_{c}
is the peripheral subgroup of
v_{c}
(see
Notations 7.1
)

Proposition 7.57
.

Suppose that for every turn
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{T}_{0}
, the element
\mathbf{g}
belongs
K_{c}
and
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}=\bar{\mathbf{e}}
.
Then the link of
v_{c}
in
S_{\Lambda}
consists of a single
L_{c}
-orbit.
Moreover the pointwise stabilizer
N_{c}
of
\bar{P}(c)
has finite index in
L_{c}
.

Proof.

The first step of the proof is to prove that
\mathscr{T}=\mathscr{T}_{0}
.
Let
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{T}_{0}
be a turn at
c
.
By assumption, we have
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}=\bar{\mathbf{e}}
.
Nevertheless,
\mathbf{e}\mathbf{g}\mathbf{e}^{\prime}
is a subword of a normal form
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{U}
.
In particular,
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})
is not reduced, hence
\mathbf{g}
does not belong to
N_{c}
.
By
Lemma 7.10
, there exists a finite number
r\in(0,\rho]
such that
\operatorname{Fix}(\mathbf{g})=\bar{P}(c,r)
.
We claim that
r=\rho
(in particular
\rho
is finite).
Assume on the contrary that
r<\rho
.
In view of
Corollary 7.14
, the image of
\mathbf{g}
in
Q_{c}=L_{c}/N_{c}
has infinite order.
Recall that
K_{c}
is the pre-image of the torsion part of
Q_{c}
.
Hence
\mathbf{g}
does not belongs to
K_{c}
.
This contradicts our assumption and completes the proof of our first claim.

We now claim that
\mathchoice{\left|y-y^{\prime}\right|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}=2\rho
, where
(y,y^{\prime})
stands for the realization of
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})
.
Since
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}=\bar{\mathbf{e}}
, we have
p_{\mathbf{e}}=p_{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}
.
According to our previous claim
\operatorname{Fix}(\mathbf{g})
is exactly
\bar{P}(c,\rho)
, which is reduced to a single point
z_{c}
satisfying
\mathchoice{\left|z_{c}-s(\mathbf{e})p_{\mathbf{e}}\right|}{|z_{c}-s(\mathbf{e})p_{\mathbf{e}}|}{|z_{c}-s(\mathbf{e})p_{\mathbf{e}}|}{|z_{c}-s(\mathbf{e})p_{\mathbf{e}}|}=\rho
.
Hence

\mathchoice{\left|s(\mathbf{e})p_{\mathbf{e}}-\mathbf{g}s(\mathbf{e})p_{\mathbf{e}}\right|}{|s(\mathbf{e})p_{\mathbf{e}}-\mathbf{g}s(\mathbf{e})p_{\mathbf{e}}|}{|s(\mathbf{e})p_{\mathbf{e}}-\mathbf{g}s(\mathbf{e})p_{\mathbf{e}}|}{|s(\mathbf{e})p_{\mathbf{e}}-\mathbf{g}s(\mathbf{e})p_{\mathbf{e}}|}=2\rho,

which completes the proof of our second claim.
As we noticed earlier,
\mathchoice{\left|z-z^{\prime}\right|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}\leqslant 2\rho
, for every realization
(z,z^{\prime})\in F
.
We get from our second claim that
F_{0}=F
and thus
\mathscr{T}_{0}=\mathscr{T}
, as we announced.

According to our assumption, for every turn
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{T}
we have
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}=\bar{\mathbf{e}}
and
\mathbf{g}\in K_{c}
.
Recall that
\bar{v}^{+}
is not a translate of
\bar{v}_{c}
.
Hence every element
h\in L
can be represented by a (non necessarily reduced) decorated circuit at
\bar{v}^{+}

\mathbf{h}=\mathbf{g}_{0}\mathbf{e}_{1}\mathbf{g}_{1}\mathbf{e}_{2}\dots\mathbf{g}_{m-1}\mathbf{e}_{m}\mathbf{g}_{m}

(20)

such that every subword
\mathbf{e}_{i}\mathbf{g}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{i+1}
corresponding to a turn at
c
belongs to
\mathscr{T}
.
Those turns always satisfy
\mathbf{e}_{i+1}=\bar{\mathbf{e}}_{i}
, which forces the link of
\bar{v}_{c}
in
\bar{S}
to be reduced to a single
L_{c}
-orbit.
Hence so is the link of
v_{c}
in
S_{\Lambda}
.
Recall that the stabilizer of every edge of
\bar{S}
adjacent to
\bar{v}_{c}
is
N_{c}
.
Consequently there is an epimorphism from
L
onto
Q_{c}=L_{c}/N_{c}
obtained by “killing” every vertex group distinct from
\bar{v}_{c}
, and every stable letter in the graph of groups decomposition induced by
\bar{S}
.
However for every turn
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{T}
, the element
\mathbf{g}
belongs to
K_{c}
.
It follows from the decomposition (
20
) that the morphism
L\twoheadrightarrow Q_{c}
maps
L
onto the torsion part of
Q_{c}
.
Since
Q_{c}=L_{c}/N_{c}
is finitely generated,
Q_{c}
is finite.
Consequently
N_{c}
has finite index in
L_{c}
.
∎

Recall that
Theorem 7.51
assumes that either the link of
v_{c}
contains two
L_{c}
-orbits or
N_{c}
has infinite index in
L_{c}
.
In view of
Proposition 7.57
, there is a turn
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{T}_{0}
such that either
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\neq\bar{\mathbf{e}}
or
\mathbf{g}\notin K_{c}
.

Proposition 7.58
and
Proposition 7.59
handle these two cases respectively.

Proposition 7.58

(Shortening by Dehn twist)
.

Assume that there exists a turn
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{T}_{0}
such that
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\neq\bar{\mathbf{e}}
.
Then there is
\ell\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
with the following property.
For every sufficiently large
k\in\mathbf{N}
, there is a Dehn twist
\alpha_{k}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{0}(L,\Lambda)
over an edge of
S_{\Lambda}
adjacent to
v
, and
\hat{\alpha}_{k}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{Aut}\left(\hat{L}_{k}\right)}{\operatorname{Aut}(\hat{L}_{k})}{\operatorname{Aut}(\hat{L}_{k})}{\operatorname{Aut}(\hat{L}_{k})}
preserving
\hat{S}_{k}
such that
\zeta_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}=\alpha_{k}\circ\zeta_{k}
and

\lambda^{+}_{1}\left(\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k},U\right)\leqslant\lambda^{+}_{1}\left(\varphi_{k},U\right)-\frac{\ell}{\varepsilon_{k}}\ \omega\text{-as}

Proof.

We denote by
(y,y^{\prime})
the realization of the turn
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})
and write
y=\lim_{\omega}y_{k}
and
y^{\prime}=\lim_{\omega}y^{\prime}_{k}
.
Fix
b\in G
such that
\eta(b)
is a non-trivial element of
N_{c}
.
According to
Lemma 7.52
, there exists a sequence
(b_{k})
of elements of
\mathchoice{\left\langle b\right\rangle}{\langle b\rangle}{\langle b\rangle}{\langle b\rangle}
with the following properties:

i.

\eta(b_{k})
belongs to
N_{c}
, for every
k\in\mathbf{N}
.

ii.

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|y_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})y^{\prime}_{k}\right|}{|y_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})y^{\prime}_{k}|}{|y_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})y^{\prime}_{k}|}{|y_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})y^{\prime}_{k}|}=0
.

iii.

if
z=\lim_{\omega}z_{k}
and
z^{\prime}=\lim_{\omega}z^{\prime}_{k}
are two points of
T
such that
(z,z^{\prime})\in F
, then for every
q\in\mathbf{Z}
,

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|z_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{q}z^{\prime}_{k}\right|}{|z_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{q}z^{\prime}_{k}|}{|z_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{q}z^{\prime}_{k}|}{|z_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{q}z^{\prime}_{k}|}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|z-z^{\prime}\right|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}.

Recall that
V_{k}
and
E_{k}
are respectively the vertex set of
D_{k,0}
and the edge set of
D_{k,1}
.
We make an abuse of notation and still denote by
\mathbf{e}
and
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}
the pre-images of
\mathbf{e}
and
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}
in
E_{k}
.
Similarly we still write
\bar{v}_{c}
for the pre-image of
\bar{v}_{c}
in
V_{k}
and
\hat{L}_{k,c}
for the vertex group
\hat{L}_{k,\bar{v}_{c}}
.
It follows from the definition of strong covers that for every sufficiently large
k\in\mathbf{N}
, the element
\hat{\eta}_{k}(b)
belongs to

s\left(\mathbf{e}\right)\hat{L}_{k,\mathbf{e}}s\left(\mathbf{e}\right)^{-1}\cap s\left(\bar{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime}\right)\hat{L}_{k,\bar{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime}}s\left(\bar{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}\subset\hat{L}_{k,c}.

Hence so does
\hat{\eta}_{k}(b_{k})
.
For such an integer
k
, we define an isomorphism
\mathbf{L}_{k}\to\mathbf{L}_{k}
, which fixes every edge in
E_{k}\setminus\{\mathbf{e},\bar{\mathbf{e}}\}
as well as the factors
\hat{L}_{k,\bar{v}}
for every
\bar{v}\in V_{k}
and maps
\mathbf{e}
to
\mathbf{e}\hat{\eta}_{k}(b_{k})
.
This morphisms induces a Dehn twist
\hat{\alpha}_{k}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{Aut}\left(\hat{L}_{k}\right)}{\operatorname{Aut}(\hat{L}_{k})}{\operatorname{Aut}(\hat{L}_{k})}{\operatorname{Aut}(\hat{L}_{k})}
.
The Dehn twists
\alpha_{k}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{0}(L,\Lambda)
are defined in a similar way so that the following diagram commutes.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{L}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{L}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\hat{\alpha}_{k}
\alpha_{k}

We now study the effect of this Dehn twist on the generating set
\eta(U)
of
L
.
Let
u\in U
.
Recall that
\bar{v}^{+}
is not in the orbit of
\bar{v}_{c}
.
Hence, the normal form
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{U}
of
\eta(u)
can be written

\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{h}_{0}(\mathbf{e}_{1}\mathbf{g}_{1}\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{1})\mathbf{h}_{1}(\mathbf{e}_{2}\mathbf{g}_{2}\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{2})\mathbf{h}_{2}\dots(\mathbf{e}_{m}\mathbf{g}_{m}\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{m})\mathbf{h}_{m}

where

belongs to
\mathscr{T}
, each

fixes a vertex in
V
different from
\bar{v}_{c}
.
For each index
i
, we write
p_{i}
( respectively
p^{\prime}_{i}
) for the attaching point in
T
of the edge
\mathbf{e}_{i}
( respectively
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{i}
) in the graph of actions
\Lambda
.
In addition we let

z_{i}=s(\mathbf{e}_{i})p_{i},\quad z^{\prime}_{i}=s(\bar{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime}_{i})p^{\prime}_{i},\quad x_{i}=s(\bar{\mathbf{e}}_{i})p_{i},\quad\text{and}\quad x^{\prime}_{i}=s(\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{i})p^{\prime}_{i}.

In particular,
(z_{i},\mathbf{g}_{i}z^{\prime}_{i})
is the realization of
(\mathbf{e}_{i},\mathbf{g}_{i},\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{i})
.
The normal form of
\eta(u)
can be used to decompose the geodesic
\mathchoice{\left[o,\eta(u)o\right]}{[o,\eta(u)o]}{[o,\eta(u)o]}{[o,\eta(u)o]}\subset T
.
More precisely applying (
11
) we get

\mathchoice{\left|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}\right|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}=\mathchoice{\left|o^{+}-\mathbf{h}_{0}x_{0}\right|}{|o^{+}-\mathbf{h}_{0}x_{0}|}{|o^{+}-\mathbf{h}_{0}x_{0}|}{|o^{+}-\mathbf{h}_{0}x_{0}|}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathchoice{\left|z_{i}-\mathbf{g}_{i}z^{\prime}_{i}\right|}{|z_{i}-\mathbf{g}_{i}z^{\prime}_{i}|}{|z_{i}-\mathbf{g}_{i}z^{\prime}_{i}|}{|z_{i}-\mathbf{g}_{i}z^{\prime}_{i}|}+\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}\mathchoice{\left|x^{\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{h}_{i}x_{i+1}\right|}{|x^{\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{h}_{i}x_{i+1}|}{|x^{\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{h}_{i}x_{i+1}|}{|x^{\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{h}_{i}x_{i+1}|}+\mathchoice{\left|x^{\prime}_{m}-\mathbf{h}_{m}o^{+}\right|}{|x^{\prime}_{m}-\mathbf{h}_{m}o^{+}|}{|x^{\prime}_{m}-\mathbf{h}_{m}o^{+}|}{|x^{\prime}_{m}-\mathbf{h}_{m}o^{+}|}.

(21)

Let us now apply the modular automorphism
\alpha_{k}
. For every
i\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket
we choose a pre-image
h_{i}
of
\mathbf{h}_{i}
in
G
.
By construction
\mathbf{h}_{i}
belongs to the vertex group
L_{\bar{w}_{i}}
for some
\bar{w}_{i}\in V\setminus\{\bar{v}_{c}\}
.
If
k
is a sufficiently large integer, then

\hat{\eta}_{k}(h_{i})\in\hat{L}_{k,\bar{w}_{i}}

where we have identified
\bar{w}_{i}\in V
with its unique pre-image in
V_{k}
.
Similarly we choose for each
i\in\llbracket 1,m\rrbracket
a pre-image
g_{i}
of
\mathbf{g}_{i}
in
G
such that
\hat{\eta}_{k}(g_{i})
belongs to
\hat{L}_{k,c}
, for all but finitely many

.
Consequently,

\mathbf{u}_{k}=\hat{\eta}_{k}(h_{0})\left[\mathbf{e}_{1}\hat{\eta}_{k}(g_{1})\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{1}\right]\hat{\eta}_{k}(h_{1})\cdots\left[\mathbf{e}_{m}\hat{\eta}_{k}(g_{m})\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{m}\right]\hat{\eta}_{k}(h_{m})

is a normal form in
\mathbf{L}_{k}
of
\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)
, provided
k
is large enough (as above, we identified the edges of
E
with their pre-images in
E_{k}
).
Consequently a pre-image in
\mathbf{L}_{k}
of
\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)
is

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{k}=\hat{\eta}_{k}(h_{0})\left[\mathbf{e}_{1}\hat{\eta}_{k}(b_{k})^{q_{1}}\eta_{k}(g_{1})\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{1}\right]\hat{\eta}_{k}(h_{1})\cdots\\
\left[\mathbf{e}_{m}\hat{\eta}_{k}(b_{k})^{q_{m}}\hat{\eta}_{k}(g_{m})\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{m}\right]\hat{\eta}_{k}(h_{m}),

where the value of
q_{i}\in\{-1,0,1\}
depends on the edges of the turn
(\mathbf{e}_{i},\mathbf{g}_{i},\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{i})
.
Since
p_{i}
and
p^{\prime}_{i}
are points of

, we can write them as

p_{i}=\lim_{\omega}p_{k,i}\quad\text{and}\quad p^{\prime}_{i}=\lim_{\omega}p^{\prime}_{k,i}

where
p_{k,i},p^{\prime}_{k,i}\in X_{k}
.
In addition we let

\displaystyle z_{k,i}

\displaystyle=\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ s(\mathbf{e}_{i})p_{k,i},

\displaystyle z^{\prime}_{k,i}

\displaystyle=\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ s(\bar{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime}_{i})p^{\prime}_{k,i},

\displaystyle x_{k,i}

\displaystyle=\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ s(\bar{\mathbf{e}}_{i})p_{k,i},

\displaystyle x^{\prime}_{k,i}

\displaystyle=\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ s(\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{i})p^{\prime}_{k,i}.

so that

z_{i}=\lim_{\omega}z_{k,i},\quad z^{\prime}_{i}=\lim_{\omega}z^{\prime}_{k,i},\quad x_{i}=\lim_{\omega}x_{k,i},\quad\text{and}\quad x^{\prime}_{i}=\lim_{\omega}x^{\prime}_{k,i}.

Observe that our choices are consistent with the definition of
(y_{k})
and
(y^{\prime}_{k})
.
That is for instance, if
z_{i}=y
( respectively
z_{i}=y^{\prime}
), then

( respectively
z_{k,i}=y^{\prime}_{k}
) for all

.
Same with

,
x_{i}
,
x^{\prime}_{i}
.

Applying the triangle inequality in
X_{k}
as we did to establish (
11
) we observe that

\mathchoice{\left|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}\right|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|o^{+}_{k}-\varphi_{k}(h_{0})x_{k,0}\right|}{|o^{+}_{k}-\varphi_{k}(h_{0})x_{k,0}|}{|o^{+}_{k}-\varphi_{k}(h_{0})x_{k,0}|}{|o^{+}_{k}-\varphi_{k}(h_{0})x_{k,0}|}\\
+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathchoice{\left|z_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{q_{i}}\varphi_{k}(g_{i})z^{\prime}_{k,i}\right|}{|z_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{q_{i}}\varphi_{k}(g_{i})z^{\prime}_{k,i}|}{|z_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{q_{i}}\varphi_{k}(g_{i})z^{\prime}_{k,i}|}{|z_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{q_{i}}\varphi_{k}(g_{i})z^{\prime}_{k,i}|}+\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}\mathchoice{\left|x^{\prime}_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(h_{i})x_{k,i+1}\right|}{|x^{\prime}_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(h_{i})x_{k,i+1}|}{|x^{\prime}_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(h_{i})x_{k,i+1}|}{|x^{\prime}_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(h_{i})x_{k,i+1}|}\\
+\mathchoice{\left|x^{\prime}_{k,m}-\varphi_{k}(h_{m})o_{k}\right|}{|x^{\prime}_{k,m}-\varphi_{k}(h_{m})o_{k}|}{|x^{\prime}_{k,m}-\varphi_{k}(h_{m})o_{k}|}{|x^{\prime}_{k,m}-\varphi_{k}(h_{m})o_{k}|}

(22)

We now focus on each term in the sum and estimate its asymptotic behavior as
k
diverges to infinity.

(T1)

Observe first that for every

,

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|x^{\prime}_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(h_{i})x_{k,i+1}\right|}{|x^{\prime}_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(h_{i})x_{k,i+1}|}{|x^{\prime}_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(h_{i})x_{k,i+1}|}{|x^{\prime}_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(h_{i})x_{k,i+1}|}=\mathchoice{\left|x^{\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{h}_{i}x_{i+1}\right|}{|x^{\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{h}_{i}x_{i+1}|}{|x^{\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{h}_{i}x_{i+1}|}{|x^{\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{h}_{i}x_{i+1}|}.

Similarly,

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|o^{+}_{k}-\varphi_{k}(h_{0})x_{k,0}\right|}{|o^{+}_{k}-\varphi_{k}(h_{0})x_{k,0}|}{|o^{+}_{k}-\varphi_{k}(h_{0})x_{k,0}|}{|o^{+}_{k}-\varphi_{k}(h_{0})x_{k,0}|}=\mathchoice{\left|o^{+}-\mathbf{h}_{0}x_{0}\right|}{|o^{+}-\mathbf{h}_{0}x_{0}|}{|o^{+}-\mathbf{h}_{0}x_{0}|}{|o^{+}-\mathbf{h}_{0}x_{0}|}

and

(T2)

Let

and consider the turn

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|z_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{q_{i}}\varphi_{k}(g_{i})z^{\prime}_{k,i}\right|}{|z_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{q_{i}}\varphi_{k}(g_{i})z^{\prime}_{k,i}|}{|z_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{q_{i}}\varphi_{k}(g_{i})z^{\prime}_{k,i}|}{|z_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{q_{i}}\varphi_{k}(g_{i})z^{\prime}_{k,i}|}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|z_{i}-\mathbf{g}_{i}z^{\prime}_{i}\right|}{|z_{i}-\mathbf{g}_{i}z^{\prime}_{i}|}{|z_{i}-\mathbf{g}_{i}z^{\prime}_{i}|}{|z_{i}-\mathbf{g}_{i}z^{\prime}_{i}|}.

Combining these observations with (

) we get that for every

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}\right|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}\right|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}.

(23)

By assumption there is at least one element

(24)

Proposition 7.59

(Shortening by peripheral automorphism)
.

Assume that there exists a turn
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{T}_{0}
and such that
\mathbf{g}\notin K_{c}

Then there is
\ell\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
with the following property.
For every sufficiently large
k\in\mathbf{N}
, there is a generalized Dehn twist
\alpha_{k}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{0}(L,\Lambda)
, and
\hat{\alpha}_{k}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{Aut}\left(\hat{L}_{k}\right)}{\operatorname{Aut}(\hat{L}_{k})}{\operatorname{Aut}(\hat{L}_{k})}{\operatorname{Aut}(\hat{L}_{k})}
preserving
\hat{S}_{k}
such that
\zeta_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}=\alpha_{k}\circ\zeta_{k}
and

\lambda^{+}_{1}\left(\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k},U\right)\leqslant\lambda^{+}_{1}\left(\varphi_{k},U\right)-\frac{\ell}{\varepsilon_{k}}\ \omega\text{-as}

Proof.

Recall that
K_{c}
is the pre-image of the torsion part of
Q_{c}=L_{c}/N_{c}
.
Hence
L_{c}
decomposes as
L_{c}=A_{c}\oplus K_{c}
where
A_{c}
is a non-trivial, free abelian group.
We make an abuse of notation and still write
\bar{v}_{c}
for the pre-image of
\bar{v}_{c}
in
V_{k}
.
Similarly, we write
\hat{L}_{k,c}
for the vertex group
\hat{L}_{k,\bar{v}_{c}}
associated to the vertex
\bar{v}_{c}\in\bar{S}_{k}
.
Recall that
L_{c}
is finitely generated relative to
N_{c}
(
Corollary 7.37
).
In particular,
A_{c}
is finitely generated.
We fix a basis
a_{0},\dots,a_{r}
of
A_{c}
, and write
\tilde{a}_{0},\dots,\tilde{a}_{r}
for pre-images of these elements in
G
.
Without loss of generality we can choose our basis so that
\mathbf{g}
decomposes as
\mathbf{g}=ma_{0}^{n}
where
m\in K_{c}
and
n\in\mathbf{N}\setminus\{0\}
. Fix
b\in G
such that
\eta(b)
is a non-trivial element of
N_{c}
.
According to
Lemma 7.52
, there exists a sequence
(b_{k})
of elements of
\mathchoice{\left\langle b\right\rangle}{\langle b\rangle}{\langle b\rangle}{\langle b\rangle}
with the following properties:

A.

\eta(b_{k})
belongs to
N_{c}
, for every
k\in\mathbf{N}
.

B.

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|y_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b^{n}_{k})y^{\prime}_{k}\right|}{|y_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b^{n}_{k})y^{\prime}_{k}|}{|y_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b^{n}_{k})y^{\prime}_{k}|}{|y_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b^{n}_{k})y^{\prime}_{k}|}=0
.

C.

if
z=\lim_{\omega}z_{k}
and
z^{\prime}=\lim_{\omega}z^{\prime}_{k}
are two points of
T
such that
(z,z^{\prime})\in F
, then for every
q\in\mathbf{Z}
,

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|z_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b^{q}_{k})z^{\prime}_{k}\right|}{|z_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b^{q}_{k})z^{\prime}_{k}|}{|z_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b^{q}_{k})z^{\prime}_{k}|}{|z_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b^{q}_{k})z^{\prime}_{k}|}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|z-z^{\prime}\right|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}.

It follows from the definition of strong covers that for every sufficiently large
k\in\mathbf{N}
, the (abelian) group
\hat{L}_{k,c}
admits a non trivial splitting
\hat{L}_{k,c}=A_{k,c}\oplus K_{k,c}
where

—

the map
\zeta_{k}\colon\hat{L}_{k}\to L
induces a isomorphism from
A_{k,c}
onto
A_{c}
and maps
K_{k,c}
into
K_{c}
;

—

the pre-image
\hat{\eta}_{k}(b_{k})\in L_{k}
of
\eta(b_{k})\in L
belongs to
K_{k,c}
.

We denote by
a_{j,k}=\hat{\eta}_{k}(\tilde{a}_{j})
for every
j\in\llbracket 0,r\rrbracket
.
Note that
a_{0,k},\dots,a_{r,k}
is a free basis of
A_{k,c}

\omega
-as.
We define an automorphism
\hat{\alpha}_{k}
of
\hat{L}_{k,c}
as follows: it fixes
K_{k,c}
as well as
a_{1,k},\dots,a_{r,k}
and maps
a_{0,k}
to
a_{0,k}\hat{\eta}_{k}(b_{k})
.
Observe that
\hat{\alpha}_{k}
is trivial when restricted to the stabilizer
\hat{L}_{k,e}
of any edge of
\bar{S}_{k}
adjacent to
\bar{v}_{c}
.
Indeed by definition of strong cover the map
\zeta_{k}\colon\hat{L}_{k}\to L
maps
\hat{L}_{k,e}
into the stabilizer of some edge of
\bar{S}
adjacent to
\bar{v}_{c}
, that is into
N_{c}
.
However
N_{c}
is contained in the factor
K_{c}
, hence
\hat{L}_{k,e}
necessarily belongs to
K_{k,c}
.
Consequently
\hat{\alpha}_{k}
is trivial when restricted to
\hat{L}_{k,e}
.
It follows that
\hat{\alpha}_{k}
extends to an automorphism of
\hat{L}_{k}
, that we still denote by
\hat{\alpha}_{k}
, that preserves the graph group decomposition of
\bar{S}_{k}
.

The automorphisms
\alpha_{k}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{0}(L,\Lambda)
are defined in a similar way so that the following diagram commutes.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{L}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{L}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\hat{\alpha}_{k}
\alpha_{k}

We now study the effect of this automorphism on the generating set of
L
.
We follow the same strategy as in the proof of the Dehn twist case.
Let
u\in U
and
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{U}
its normal form that we write

\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{h}_{0}(\mathbf{e}_{1}\mathbf{g}_{1}\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{1})\mathbf{h}_{1}(\mathbf{e}_{2}\mathbf{g}_{2}\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{2})\mathbf{h}_{2}\dots(\mathbf{e}_{m}\mathbf{g}_{m}\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{m})\mathbf{h}_{m}

, each

Just as before we have

\mathchoice{\left|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}\right|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}=\mathchoice{\left|o^{+}-\mathbf{h}_{0}x_{0}\right|}{|o^{+}-\mathbf{h}_{0}x_{0}|}{|o^{+}-\mathbf{h}_{0}x_{0}|}{|o^{+}-\mathbf{h}_{0}x_{0}|}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathchoice{\left|z_{i}-\mathbf{g}_{i}z^{\prime}_{i}\right|}{|z_{i}-\mathbf{g}_{i}z^{\prime}_{i}|}{|z_{i}-\mathbf{g}_{i}z^{\prime}_{i}|}{|z_{i}-\mathbf{g}_{i}z^{\prime}_{i}|}+\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}\mathchoice{\left|x^{\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{h}_{i}x_{i+1}\right|}{|x^{\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{h}_{i}x_{i+1}|}{|x^{\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{h}_{i}x_{i+1}|}{|x^{\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{h}_{i}x_{i+1}|}+\mathchoice{\left|x^{\prime}_{m}-\mathbf{h}_{m}o^{+}\right|}{|x^{\prime}_{m}-\mathbf{h}_{m}o^{+}|}{|x^{\prime}_{m}-\mathbf{h}_{m}o^{+}|}{|x^{\prime}_{m}-\mathbf{h}_{m}o^{+}|}.

(25)

Let us now apply the modular automorphism

. For every

\omega

\mathbf{u}_{k}=\hat{\eta}_{k}(h_{0})\left[\mathbf{e}_{1}\hat{\eta}_{k}(g_{1})\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{1}\right]\hat{\eta}_{k}(h_{1})\cdots\left[\mathbf{e}_{m}\hat{\eta}_{k}(g_{m})\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{m}\right]\hat{\eta}_{k}(h_{m})

so that

As previously we have the following estimate,

\mathchoice{\left|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}\right|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|o^{+}_{k}-\varphi_{k}(h_{0})x_{k,0}\right|}{|o^{+}_{k}-\varphi_{k}(h_{0})x_{k,0}|}{|o^{+}_{k}-\varphi_{k}(h_{0})x_{k,0}|}{|o^{+}_{k}-\varphi_{k}(h_{0})x_{k,0}|}\\

(26)

Moreover we prove that for every
i\in\llbracket 1,m-1\rrbracket
,

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|x^{\prime}_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(h_{i})x_{k,i+1}\right|}{|x^{\prime}_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(h_{i})x_{k,i+1}|}{|x^{\prime}_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(h_{i})x_{k,i+1}|}{|x^{\prime}_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(h_{i})x_{k,i+1}|}=\mathchoice{\left|x^{\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{h}_{i}x_{i+1}\right|}{|x^{\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{h}_{i}x_{i+1}|}{|x^{\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{h}_{i}x_{i+1}|}{|x^{\prime}_{i}-\mathbf{h}_{i}x_{i+1}|}.

while,

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|o^{+}_{k}-\varphi_{k}(h_{0})x_{k,0}\right|}{|o^{+}_{k}-\varphi_{k}(h_{0})x_{k,0}|}{|o^{+}_{k}-\varphi_{k}(h_{0})x_{k,0}|}{|o^{+}_{k}-\varphi_{k}(h_{0})x_{k,0}|}=\mathchoice{\left|o^{+}-\mathbf{h}_{0}x_{0}\right|}{|o^{+}-\mathbf{h}_{0}x_{0}|}{|o^{+}-\mathbf{h}_{0}x_{0}|}{|o^{+}-\mathbf{h}_{0}x_{0}|}

and

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|x^{\prime}_{k,m}-\varphi_{k}(h_{m})o_{k}\right|}{|x^{\prime}_{k,m}-\varphi_{k}(h_{m})o_{k}|}{|x^{\prime}_{k,m}-\varphi_{k}(h_{m})o_{k}|}{|x^{\prime}_{k,m}-\varphi_{k}(h_{m})o_{k}|}=\mathchoice{\left|x^{\prime}_{m}-\mathbf{h}_{m}o^{+}\right|}{|x^{\prime}_{m}-\mathbf{h}_{m}o^{+}|}{|x^{\prime}_{m}-\mathbf{h}_{m}o^{+}|}{|x^{\prime}_{m}-\mathbf{h}_{m}o^{+}|}.

Let
i\in\llbracket 1,m\rrbracket
and consider the turn
(\mathbf{e}_{i},\mathbf{g}_{i},\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{i})
.
Recall that the pair
(z_{i},\mathbf{g}_{i}z^{\prime}_{i})
is the realization of
(\mathbf{e}_{i},\mathbf{g}_{i},\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{i})
and thus belongs to
F
. It follows then from our choice of
b_{k}
that

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|z_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{q_{i}}\varphi_{k}(g_{i})z^{\prime}_{k,i}\right|}{|z_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{q_{i}}\varphi_{k}(g_{i})z^{\prime}_{k,i}|}{|z_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{q_{i}}\varphi_{k}(g_{i})z^{\prime}_{k,i}|}{|z_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{q_{i}}\varphi_{k}(g_{i})z^{\prime}_{k,i}|}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|z_{i}-\mathbf{g}_{i}z^{\prime}_{i}\right|}{|z_{i}-\mathbf{g}_{i}z^{\prime}_{i}|}{|z_{i}-\mathbf{g}_{i}z^{\prime}_{i}|}{|z_{i}-\mathbf{g}_{i}z^{\prime}_{i}|}.

Combining these observations with (
25
) and (
26
) we get that for every
u\in U
,

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}\right|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}\right|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}.

(27)

The previous computation holds for every
u\in U
.
However, by assumption there is at least one element
u\in U
and one turn
(\mathbf{e}_{i},\mathbf{g}_{i},\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{i})
in its normal form that coincides with the turn
(\mathbf{e},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})
fixed at the beginning.
By construction it satisfies
q_{i}=n
(this is the way we defined
n
at the beginning of the proof).
For this turn, our choice of
b_{k}
gives

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|z_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{n}\varphi_{k}(g_{i})z^{\prime}_{k,i}\right|}{|z_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{n}\varphi_{k}(g_{i})z^{\prime}_{k,i}|}{|z_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{n}\varphi_{k}(g_{i})z^{\prime}_{k,i}|}{|z_{k,i}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{n}\varphi_{k}(g_{i})z^{\prime}_{k,i}|}=\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|y_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{n}y^{\prime}_{k}\right|}{|y_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{n}y^{\prime}_{k}|}{|y_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{n}y^{\prime}_{k}|}{|y_{k}-\varphi_{k}(b_{k})^{n}y^{\prime}_{k}|}=0.

Hence this specific element
u
satisfies

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}\right|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}\right|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}-\mathchoice{\left|y-y^{\prime}\right|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}.

(28)

The rest of the proof goes as in the proof of
Proposition 7.58
.
We conclude that

\lambda^{+}_{1}(\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k},U)\leqslant\lim_{\omega}\lambda_{1}(\varphi_{k},U)-\frac{\mathchoice{\left|y-y^{\prime}\right|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}}{\varepsilon_{k}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{k}}\right).\qed

Proof of
Theorem 7.51
.

We now summarize the above study.
Fix a peripheral vertex
v\in S_{\Lambda}
.
If we collapse all edges of
S_{\Lambda}
which are not adjacent to a vertex in the
L
-orbit of
v_{c}
, we get a new tree
\bar{S}
which is the “smallest”
L
-tree compatible is compatible with
S_{\Lambda}
and “sees”
L_{c}
as a vertex group.
This tree
\bar{S}
can also bee seen as a graph of group decomposition of
L
, providing a normal form to express the elements of
L
(as explained in the beginning of this section).
A turn is now a subword of the form
\mathbf{e}\mathbf{g}\mathbf{e}^{\prime}
, where
\mathbf{g}
belongs to
L_{c}
, which appears in the normal form an element of
L
(see
Definition 7.53
).
Among all possible turns showing up in the normal form of the generators
u\in U
of
L
, we pay a particular assumption to the subset
\mathscr{T}_{0}
which consists of all turns whose “angle at
c
” is roughly speaking minimal (see
Definition 7.56
).
In view of Assumptions
(C2)((A3))(A)
and
(C2)((A3))(B)
,
Proposition 7.57
tells us that there is a turn
\mathbf{e}\mathbf{g}\mathbf{e}^{\prime}
in
\mathscr{T}_{0}
such that one of the following holds:

A.

either
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\neq\bar{\mathbf{e}}
, or

B.

\mathbf{g}
does not belong to
K_{c}
.

In the former case, we can use
Proposition 7.58
to shorten the sequence
(\varphi_{k})
with a sequence
(\alpha_{k})
of Dehn twists around an edge adjacent to
v
.
In the latter case, the shortening is obtained by
Proposition 7.59
using generalized Dehn twists.
∎

7.5.3
Simplicial transverse component

Lemma 7.60
.

Let
x=\lim_{\omega}x_{k}
and
y=\lim_{\omega}y_{k}
be two points of
X_{\omega}
.
Assume that
\mathchoice{\left[x,y\right]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}
is a transverse arc.
Let
g\in G
such that
\eta(g)
is a non-trivial element fixing pointwise
[x,y]
.
There exists a sequence
(g_{k})
of elements in
\mathchoice{\left\langle g\right\rangle}{\langle g\rangle}{\langle g\rangle}{\langle g\rangle}
, such that

A.

\eta(g_{k})
pointwise fixes
\mathchoice{\left[x,y\right]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}

\omega
-as;

B.

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|x_{k}-\varphi_{k}(g_{k})y_{k}\right|}{|x_{k}-\varphi_{k}(g_{k})y_{k}|}{|x_{k}-\varphi_{k}(g_{k})y_{k}|}{|x_{k}-\varphi_{k}(g_{k})y_{k}|}=0
.

Proof.

Since
\mathchoice{\left[x,y\right]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}
is non-degenerate,
\eta(g)
is not elusive (
Lemma 7.9
).
On the other hand, as
\mathchoice{\left[x,y\right]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}
is transverse,
\eta(g)
does not belong to
L_{c}
for some
c\in\mathcal{C}_{\omega}
(
Lemma 7.10
).
It follows that
\varphi_{k}(g)
is loxodromic
\omega
-as.
There exists a sequence
(d_{k})
converging to zero such that
x_{k},y_{k}\in\operatorname{Fix}(\varphi_{k}(g),d_{k})

\omega
-as.
In particular,
\mathchoice{\left\|\varphi_{k}(g)\right\|}{\|\varphi_{k}(g)\|}{\|\varphi_{k}(g)\|}{\|\varphi_{k}(g)\|}\leqslant d_{k}
.
Since
\varphi_{k}(g)
is
\delta_{k}
-thin,
x_{k}
and
y_{k}
lie in the
(d_{k}/2+50\delta_{k})
-neighborhood of any
100\delta_{k}
-local
(1,\delta_{k})
-quasi-geodesic of
\varepsilon_{k}X_{k}
joining the attractive and repulsive points of
\varphi_{k}(g)
.
Recall that
\varphi_{k}(g)
approximately acts on this geodesic by translation of length
\mathchoice{\left\|\varphi_{k}(g)\right\|}{\|\varphi_{k}(g)\|}{\|\varphi_{k}(g)\|}{\|\varphi_{k}(g)\|}
.
Consequently there exists
g_{k}\in\mathchoice{\left\langle g\right\rangle}{\langle g\rangle}{\langle g\rangle}{\langle g\rangle}
such that

\mathchoice{\left|x_{k}-\varphi_{k}\left(g_{k}\right)y_{k}\right|}{|x_{k}-\varphi_{k}\left(g_{k}\right)y_{k}|}{|x_{k}-\varphi_{k}\left(g_{k}\right)y_{k}|}{|x_{k}-\varphi_{k}\left(g_{k}\right)y_{k}|}\leqslant 2d_{k}+1000\delta_{k}\quad\omega\text{-as}.

In particular,
\mathchoice{\left|x_{k}-\varphi_{k}(g_{k})y_{k}\right|}{|x_{k}-\varphi_{k}(g_{k})y_{k}|}{|x_{k}-\varphi_{k}(g_{k})y_{k}|}{|x_{k}-\varphi_{k}(g_{k})y_{k}|}
converges to zero.
Since
\eta(g_{k})
belongs to
\mathchoice{\left\langle\eta(g)\right\rangle}{\langle\eta(g)\rangle}{\langle\eta(g)\rangle}{\langle\eta(g)\rangle}
, it pointwise fixes
\mathchoice{\left[x,y\right]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}
, whence the result.
∎

Proposition 7.61
.

Assume that
T
contains a transverse simplicial arc.
Then there exists
\ell>0
such that for every sufficiently large
k\in\mathbf{N}
, there are
\alpha_{k}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{0}(L,S)
and
\hat{\alpha}_{k}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{Aut}\left(\hat{L}_{k}\right)}{\operatorname{Aut}(\hat{L}_{k})}{\operatorname{Aut}(\hat{L}_{k})}{\operatorname{Aut}(\hat{L}_{k})}
preserving
\hat{S}_{k}
such that
\zeta_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}=\alpha_{k}\circ\zeta_{k}
and

\lambda_{1}\left(\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k},U\right)<\lambda_{1}\left(\varphi_{k},U\right)-\frac{\ell}{\varepsilon_{k}}\ \omega\text{-as}.

Proof.

The strategy in this situation has been detailed many times in the literature.
We refer the reader to Rips and Sela
[
47
, Section 6]
, Perin
[
46
, Section 5.6]
, or Weidmann-Reinfeldt
[
58
, Section 5.2.3]
for a carefully written proof.
Let us just highlight its main steps.
Let
x=\lim_{\omega}x_{k}
and
y=\lim_{\omega}y_{k}
such that
\mathchoice{\left[x,y\right]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}
is a maximal simplicial arc of
T
contained in a transverse subtree.
Recall that
S
is the simplicial tree obtained by refining the skeleton of the graph of actions
\Lambda
.
We denote by
\bar{S}
the one-edge splitting obtained from
S
be collapsing every edge orbit but the edge
e
corresponding to
\mathchoice{\left[x,y\right]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}
.
Note that the subgroup
L_{e}
is non-trivial otherwise
L
would be freely decomposable relative to
\mathcal{H}
.
Using
Lemma 7.60
we build a sequence
(g_{k})
of elements of
G
such that

A.

\eta(g_{k})
pointwise fixes
\mathchoice{\left[x,y\right]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}

\omega
-as, in particular
\eta(g_{k})
belongs to
L_{e}
;

B.

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|x_{k}-\varphi_{k}(g_{k})y_{k}\right|}{|x_{k}-\varphi_{k}(g_{k})y_{k}|}{|x_{k}-\varphi_{k}(g_{k})y_{k}|}{|x_{k}-\varphi_{k}(g_{k})y_{k}|}=0
.

For every
k\in\mathbf{N}
, we define a Dehn twist
\alpha_{k}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{0}(L,S)
over
e
by mapping the edge
e
to
e\eta(g_{k})
.
We write
\hat{\alpha}_{k}
for the automorphism of
\hat{L}_{k}
lifting
\alpha_{k}
(see
Lemma 7.47
).
Proceeding as in the case of peripheral component (see
Section 7.5.2
) we prove that for every
u\in U
,

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}\right|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}\right|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}

However, since
U
generates
L
, there is at least one element
u\in U
such that the geodesic
\mathchoice{\left[o^{+},uo^{+}\right]}{[o^{+},uo^{+}]}{[o^{+},uo^{+}]}{[o^{+},uo^{+}]}
contains a translated copy of
\mathchoice{\left[x,y\right]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}
.
For such an element we get

\lim_{\omega}\mathchoice{\left|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}\right|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}{|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k}(u)o^{+}_{k}-o^{+}_{k}|}\leqslant\mathchoice{\left|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}\right|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}{|\eta(u)o^{+}-o^{+}|}-\mathchoice{\left|x-y\right|}{|x-y|}{|x-y|}{|x-y|}.

It follows from there that

\lambda^{+}_{1}\left(\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\eta_{k},U\right)\leqslant\lambda^{+}_{1}\left(\varphi_{k},U\right)-\frac{\mathchoice{\left|x-y\right|}{|x-y|}{|x-y|}{|x-y|}}{\varepsilon_{k}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{k}}\right).\qed

Remark
.

We keep the notation of the proof.
If the point
o^{+}
lies on the arc
\mathchoice{\left[x,y\right]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}{[x,y]}
, we need to subdivide this arc first, but it does not change the strategy.

In
[
47
]
or
[
58
]
the authors only consider limit groups over a fixed hyperbolic group.
The proof of Perin
[
46
]
does not make this assumption.
However it requires uniform control on
L_{e}
.
More precisely she asks that there exists
g\in G
in the pre-image of
L_{e}
such that
\mathchoice{\left\|\varphi_{k}(g)\right\|}{\|\varphi_{k}(g)\|}{\|\varphi_{k}(g)\|}{\|\varphi_{k}(g)\|}>12\delta_{k}

\omega
-as.
This assumption is used to prove the analogue of our
Lemma 7.60
see
[
46
, Lemma 5.25]
.
In our context, given a triple
(X,\Gamma,\mathcal{C})\in\mathfrak{H}_{\delta}(\tilde{\rho}_{k})
, we have no control on the injectivity radius of the action of
\Gamma
on
X
.
Consequently such a uniform control does not hold in general.
However, as we have seen in the proof of
Lemma 7.60
, it can be advantageously replaced by the fact that hyperbolic isometries of
\Gamma
are uniformly thin.

7.5.4
Proof of the shortening argument

We are now in position to complete the proof of the shortening argument.

End of the proof of
Theorem 7.46
.

By assumption the image of
\varphi_{k}\colon G\to\Gamma_{k}
is not abelian
\omega
-as (otherwise so would be
L
).
Applying
Lemma 3.8
, we get

\frac{1}{2\left|U\right|}\lambda_{1}^{+}(\varphi_{k},U)\leqslant\lambda_{\infty}(\varphi_{k},U)\leqslant\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{k}}.

Hence it suffices to prove that there exist
\ell>0
and two collections of automorphisms
\alpha_{k}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{0}(L,S)
and
\hat{\alpha}_{k}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{Aut}\left(\hat{L}_{k}\right)}{\operatorname{Aut}(\hat{L}_{k})}{\operatorname{Aut}(\hat{L}_{k})}{\operatorname{Aut}(\hat{L}_{k})}
preserving
\hat{S}_{k}
such that
\zeta_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}=\alpha_{k}\circ\zeta_{k}
and satisfying

\lambda^{+}_{1}\left(\hat{\varphi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\eta}_{k},U\right)\leqslant\lambda^{+}_{1}\left(\varphi_{k},U\right)-\frac{\ell}{\varepsilon_{k}}\ \omega\text{-as}.

Assume that such a statement does not hold.
In view of Propositions
7.50
and
7.61
, every transverse component of
T
is reduced to a point.
Said differently,
T
is covered by its peripheral components.
It follows from
Theorem 7.51
that every vertex of
S/L
corresponding to a peripheral subtree has valence one.
In other words the graph of groups decomposition associated to
S
has the following form
B
L_{c_{1}}
L_{c_{2}}
L_{c_{3}}
\vdots
L_{c_{m}}
N_{1}
N_{2}
N_{3}
N_{m}

where
B
is the stabilizer of a transverse component,
c_{1},\dots,c_{m}\in\mathcal{C}
is a collection of
L
-representatives of
\mathcal{C}
, and
N_{i}
is the pointwise stabilizer of
\bar{P}(c_{i})
for every
i\in\llbracket 1,m\rrbracket
.
According to
Theorem 7.51
,
N_{i}
has finite index in
L_{c_{i}}
.
This means that
S
is a generalized root splitting, which contradicts our assumption.
∎

8
Lifting theorems

In this section we study when a morphism
\varphi\colon G\to\Gamma/\Gamma^{n}
can be lifted to a morphism
\tilde{\varphi}\colon G\to\Gamma
.
As we noticed in the introduction, this property heavily depends on the group
G
.
We prove three lifting statements that can be used in different contexts.
The first (and easiest) one applies when no quotient of
G
has an abelian splitting (
Corollary 8.4
).
It serves mostly as a warm up for the next results.
The second proposition deals with groups
G
without essential root-splitting (
Corollary 8.7
) and the last one pushes, when it is possible, all the standard arguments on limit groups (
Corollary 8.16
).

Optimal approximation.

Our first task is to define a suitable notion of short morphisms for the study of limit groups over the periodic quotients of some hyperbolic group.

From now on the parameter
\delta\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
and the map
\rho\colon\mathbf{N}\to\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
are fixed as in
Theorem 3.11
.
Let
\tau\in(0,1)
.
We write
N_{\tau}
for the critical exponent provided by
Theorem 3.11
.
Let
n\geqslant N_{\tau}
be an odd integer.
Recall that a
\tau
-bootstrap for the exponent
n
– introduced in
Definition 3.9
– is a pair
(\Gamma,X)
designed to serve as the initial step to approximate the
n
-periodic quotients of the group
\Gamma
.
We fix such a bootstrap, say
(\Gamma,X)
.
We denote by
(\Gamma_{j},X_{j},\mathcal{C}_{j})
the approximating sequence given by
Theorem 3.11
to study
\Gamma/\Gamma^{n}
.
By construction all these triples belong to the class
\mathfrak{H}_{\delta}(\rho(n))
provided by
Definition 3.2
.

Let
G
be a group and
U
a finite subset of
G
.
Let
\varphi\colon G\to\Gamma/\Gamma^{n}
.
Recall that an approximation of
\varphi
relative to
U
is a morphism
\tilde{\varphi}\colon G\to\Gamma_{j}
lifting
\varphi
such that
U\cap\ker\tilde{\varphi}=U\cap\ker\varphi
(see
Definition 3.13
).
Such an approximation is minimal if
j
is minimal for the above property.

Definition 8.1
.

Let
\varepsilon\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
.
Let
G
be a group generated by a finite set
U
and
V
a subset of
G
.
Let
\mathcal{P}\subset{\rm Hom}(G,\Gamma/\Gamma^{n})
be a set of morphisms from
G
to
\Gamma/\Gamma^{n}
.
We say that a morphism
\varphi\in\mathcal{P}
is
\varepsilon
-short among
\mathcal{P}
relative to
V
if it admits a minimal approximation
\varphi_{i}\colon G\to\Gamma_{i}
relative to
V
such that for every
\psi\in\mathcal{P}
, for every minimal approximation
\psi_{j}\colon G\to\Gamma_{j}
of
\psi
relative to
V
,

—

either
i<j
,

—

or
i=j
and
\lambda_{1}^{+}(\varphi_{i},U)\leqslant\lambda_{1}^{+}(\psi_{j},U)+\varepsilon
.

In this context
\varphi_{i}
is called an
optimal approximation
of
\varphi
.

Remark 8.2
.

For our purpose we are interested in limit groups marked by a finitely generated group
G
.
If
F
is a finitely generated free group, recall that any projection
\pi\colon F\twoheadrightarrow G
induces an embedding
\mathfrak{G}(G)\to\mathfrak{G}(F)
.
We often prefer to work in
\mathfrak{G}(F)
rather than in
\mathfrak{G}(G)
.
The reason is that
G
may not be finitely presented.
In such a situation finding approximations of a morphism
G\to\Gamma/\Gamma^{n}
is not always automatic, unlike as in
Proposition 3.14
.

8.1
Rigid groups

Proposition 8.3
.

Let
F
be a finitely generated free group and
U
be a free basis of
F
.
Let
\pi\colon F\twoheadrightarrow G
be an epimorphism where
G
is a group none of whose quotients admit an abelian splitting.
Let
\tau\in(0,1)
.
There exist
\lambda\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
and a finite subset
W\subset\ker\pi
, such that for every finite subset
V\subset F
, there is a critical exponent
N\in\mathbf{N}
, with the following property.

Let
n\geqslant N
be an odd integer.
Let
(\Gamma,X)
be a
\tau
-bootstrap for the exponent
n
.
For every morphism
\varphi\colon F\to\Gamma/\Gamma^{n}
whose image is not abelian, if
W\subset\ker\varphi
, then there is a morphism
\tilde{\varphi}\colon F\to\Gamma
lifting
\varphi
relative to
V
and such that
\lambda_{\infty}(\tilde{\varphi},U)\leqslant\lambda
.

Proof.

We fix a non-decreasing exhaustion
(W_{k})
of
\ker\pi
by finite subsets.
Assume on the contrary that the statement is false.
For every
k\in\mathbf{N}
, there exists a finite subset
V_{k}\subset F
such that for every
N\in\mathbf{N}
, there are an odd integer
n\geqslant N
, a
\tau
-bootstrap
(\Gamma,X)
for the exponent
n
, and a morphism
\varphi\colon F\to\Gamma/\Gamma^{n}
whose image is not abelian, such that
W_{k}\subset\ker\varphi
, but every lift
\tilde{\varphi}\colon F\to\Gamma
of
\varphi
relative to
V_{k}
satisfies
\lambda_{\infty}(\tilde{\varphi},U)\geqslant k
.

We write
\ell_{k}
for the length of the longest element in
U_{k}=V_{k}\cup W_{k}
(seen as a word over
U
).
The map
\rho\colon\mathbf{N}\to\mathbf{R}_{+}
diverges to infinity, hence there exists
M_{k}\in\mathbf{N}
such that for every integer
n\geqslant M_{k}
we have

\frac{\rho(n)}{100\ell_{k}}\geqslant k.

In addition, we write
N_{\tau}
for the critical exponent given by
Theorem 3.11
.

By definition of
(V_{k})
, for every
k\in\mathbf{N}
, there exist
n_{k}\geqslant\max\{N_{\tau},M_{k}\}
, a
\tau
-bootstrap
(\Gamma_{k},X_{k})
for the exponent
n_{k}
and a morphism
\varphi_{k}\colon F\to\Gamma_{k}/\Gamma_{k}^{n_{k}}
whose image is not abelian, such that
W_{k}\subset\ker\varphi_{k}
, but every lift
\tilde{\varphi}_{k}\colon F\to\Gamma_{k}
of
\varphi_{k}
relative to
V_{k}
satisfies
\lambda_{\infty}(\tilde{\varphi}_{k},U)\geqslant k
.
For each
k\in\mathbf{N}
,
Theorem 3.11
, provides an approximating sequence

(\Gamma_{j,k},X_{j,k},\mathcal{C}_{j,k})_{j\in\mathbf{N}}

of
\Gamma_{k}/\Gamma_{k}^{n_{k}}
.
We denote by
\psi_{k}\colon F\to\Gamma_{j_{k},k}
a minimal approximation of
\varphi_{k}
relative to
U_{k}
(see
Definition 3.13
).
Recall that
V_{k}
is contained in
U_{k}
.
If
j_{k}=0
, then
\psi_{k}\colon F\to\Gamma
is a fortiori a lift of
\varphi_{k}
relative to
V_{k}
.
Hence
\lambda_{\infty}(\psi_{k},U)\geqslant k
.
If
j_{k}\geqslant 1
, combining
Proposition 3.14
with our choice of
M_{k}
, we get

\lambda_{\infty}(\psi_{k},U)\geqslant\frac{\rho(n_{k})}{100\ell_{k}}\geqslant k

Consequently, up to passing to a subsequence,
(\Gamma_{j_{k},k},\psi_{k})
converges to a non-abelian, divergent limit group over
\mathfrak{H}_{\delta}
, say
(L,\eta)
.
By construction for every
k\in\mathbf{N}
,

W_{k}\cap\ker\psi_{k}=W_{k}\cap\ker\varphi_{k}=W_{k}.

Consequently
L
is necessarily a quotient of
G
.
Proceeding as in
Section 7
we build an action of
L
on a limit
\mathbf{R}
-tree
T
.
This action decomposes as a graph of actions (
Theorem 7.44
).
Since
L
is non-abelian, it necessarily has a non-trivial abelian splitting, which contradicts our assumption.
∎

Notations
.

In the next statement,
\iota_{\gamma}
stands for the conjugation by
\gamma\in\Gamma
.

Corollary 8.4
.

Let
G
be a finitely generated group none of whose quotient admits an abelian splitting.
Let
\Gamma
be a non-elementary, torsion-free, hyperbolic group. There exist
N\in\mathbf{N}
and finite collection
\{\psi_{1},\dots,\psi_{p}\}
of morphisms from
G
to
\Gamma
, such that for every odd integer
n\geqslant N
, the following holds.

For every morphism
\varphi\colon G\to\Gamma/\Gamma^{n}
whose image is not abelian, there exist
i\in\llbracket 1,p\rrbracket
and
\gamma\in\Gamma
, such that
\iota_{\gamma}\circ\psi_{i}
lifts
\varphi
.

Proof.

Let
X
be Cayley graph of
\Gamma
.
It is a
\tau
-bootstrap from some
\tau\in(0,1)
(and for every exponent
n\in\mathbf{N}
).
Let
F
be the free group generated by a finite generating set
U
of
G
and
\pi\colon F\twoheadrightarrow G
the corresponding epimorphism.
Being torsion-free hyperbolic,
\Gamma
is equationally Noetherian
[
52
, Theorem 1.22]
.
Consequently there exists a finite subset
V\subset\ker\pi
with the following property:
for every morphism
\varphi\colon F\to\Gamma
, if
V\subset\ker\varphi
, then
\varphi
factors through
\pi
.
Let
\lambda\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
,
W\subset\ker\pi
and
N\in\mathbf{N}
be the parameters given by
Proposition 8.3
.
Since the action of
\Gamma
on
X
is proper and co-compact, there are finitely many morphisms
\psi_{1},\dots,\psi_{p}
from
G
to
\Gamma
such that every morphism
\psi\colon G\to\Gamma
satisfying
\lambda_{\infty}(\psi,U)\leqslant\lambda
is conjugated to one of the
\psi_{j}
.

Consider now an odd integer
n\geqslant N
and a morphism
\varphi\colon G\to\Gamma/\Gamma^{n}
whose image is not abelian.
By construction
W
is contained in
\ker\varphi\circ\pi
.
According to
Proposition 8.3
, there exists a morphism
\tilde{\mu}\colon F\to\Gamma
lifting
\mu=\varphi\circ\pi
relative to
V
such that
\lambda_{\infty}(\tilde{\mu},U)\leqslant\lambda
.
Recall that
V
is contained in
\ker\pi
, hence
V
is also contained in
\ker\tilde{\mu}
.
Consequently
\tilde{\mu}
factors through
\pi
.
The resulting morphism
\tilde{\varphi}\colon G\to\Gamma
is a lift of
\varphi
such that
\lambda_{\infty}(\tilde{\varphi},U)\leqslant\lambda
.
In particular,
\tilde{\varphi}
is conjugated to one of the
\psi_{i}
.
∎

8.2
(Almost) injective morphisms

Proposition 8.5
.

Let
F
be a finitely generated free group and
U
be a free basis of
F
.
Let
\pi\colon F\twoheadrightarrow G
be an epimorphism where
G
is a freely indecomposable group with no essential root splitting.
Let
\tau\in(0,1)
.
There exist a finite subset
W\subset G\setminus\{1\}
and
\lambda\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
, such that for every finite subset
V\subset F
, there is a critical exponent
N\in\mathbf{N}
with the following properties.

Let
n\geqslant N
be an odd integer.
Let
(\Gamma,X)
be a
\tau
-bootstrap for the exponent
n
.
For every morphism
\varphi\colon G\to\Gamma/\Gamma^{n}
whose image is not abelian, there exists
\alpha\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{Aut}\left(G\right)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}
such that one of the following holds:

—

W\cap\ker(\varphi\circ\alpha)\neq\emptyset
;

—

there is a morphism
\psi\colon F\to\Gamma
lifting
\varphi\circ\alpha\circ\pi
relative to
V
such that
\lambda_{\infty}(\psi,U)\leqslant\lambda
.

Proof.

Let
\tau\in(0,1)
.
We fix a non-decreasing exhaustion
(W_{k})
of
G\setminus\{1\}
by finite subsets.
We assume that the statement is false.
In particular, the following fact holds

Fact 8.6
.

For every
k\in\mathbf{N}
, there is a finite subset
V_{k}\subset F
such that for every
N\in\mathbf{N}
, there exist an odd integer
n\geqslant N
, a
\tau
-bootstrap
(\Gamma,X)
for the exponent
n
, and a morphism
\varphi\colon G\to\Gamma/\Gamma^{n}
whose image is not abelian such that for every
\alpha\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{Aut}\left(G\right)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}
, the following holds.

A.

W_{k}\cap\ker(\varphi\circ\alpha)=\emptyset

B.

if
\varphi\circ\alpha\circ\pi
admits a lift
\psi\colon F\to\Gamma_{k}
relative to
V_{k}
, then
\lambda_{\infty}(\psi,U)\geqslant k
.

We fix a non-decreasing exhaustion
(U_{k})
of
F
by finite subsets such that
V_{k}\subset U_{k}
for every
k\in\mathbf{N}
.
We write
\ell_{k}
for the length of the longest element in
U_{k}
(seen as a word over
U
).
Since the map
\rho\colon\mathbf{N}\to\mathbf{R}_{+}
diverges to infinity, for every
k\in\mathbf{N}
, there exists
M_{k}
such that for every
n\geqslant M_{k}
,

\frac{\rho(n)}{100\ell_{k}}\geqslant k.

Let
N_{\tau}
be the critical exponent given by
Theorem 3.11
.
For every
k\in\mathbf{N}
, there is an odd integer
n_{k}\geqslant\max\{N_{\tau},M_{k}\}
, a
\tau
-bootstrap
(\Gamma_{k},X_{k})
for the exponent
n_{k}
and a morphism
\varphi_{k}\colon G\to\Gamma_{k}/\Gamma_{k}^{n_{k}}
as in
Fact 8.6
.
For each
k\in\mathbf{N}
,
Theorem 3.11
, provides an approximating sequence

(\Gamma_{j,k},X_{j,k},\mathcal{C}_{j,k})_{j\in\mathbf{N}}

of
\Gamma_{k}/\Gamma_{k}^{n_{k}}
.
Let
\varepsilon\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
.
Up to pre-composing
\varphi_{k}
with an element of
\mathchoice{\operatorname{Aut}\left(G\right)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}
, we can assume that
\varphi_{k}\circ\pi
is
\varepsilon
-short among
\mathcal{P}_{k}=\mathchoice{\left\{\varphi_{k}\circ\alpha\circ\pi\;:\ \alpha\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{Aut}\left(G\right)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}\right\}}{\{\varphi_{k}\circ\alpha\circ\pi\;:\ \alpha\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{Aut}\left(G\right)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}\}}{\{\varphi_{k}\circ\alpha\circ\pi\;:\ \alpha\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{Aut}\left(G\right)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}\}}{\{\varphi_{k}\circ\alpha\circ\pi\;:\ \alpha\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{Aut}\left(G\right)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}\}}
relative to
U_{k}
.
We denote by
\psi_{k}\colon F\to\Gamma_{j_{k},k}
an optimal approximation of
\varphi_{k}\circ\pi
relative to
U_{k}
.

By construction
\ker\psi_{k}
is contained in
\ker(\varphi_{k}\circ\pi)
.
On the one hand, by
(C2)(T2)A
the stable kernel of
(\varphi_{k}\circ\pi)
– see (
10
) for the definition – is exactly
\ker\pi
.
On the other hand,
U_{k}\cap\ker\pi
is contained in
U_{k}\cap\ker(\varphi_{k}\circ\pi)
and therefore in
\ker\psi_{k}
for every
k\in\mathbf{N}
.
If follows that
(\Gamma_{j_{k},k},\psi_{k})
converges to
(G,\pi)
in the space of marked groups
\mathfrak{G}(F)
.
We next claim that for every
k\in\mathbf{N}
,

\lambda_{\infty}(\psi_{k},U)\geqslant k

Suppose first that
j_{k}=0
.
Since
V_{k}
is contained in
U_{k}
, the morphism
\psi_{k}
is a fortiori a lift of
\varphi_{k}\circ\pi
relative to
V_{k}
.
Thus the conclusion follows from
(C2)(T2)B
.
Suppose now that
j_{k}\geqslant 1
.
Observe that the image of
\psi_{k}
is not abelian.
Indeed otherwise so would be the one of
\varphi_{k}
.
Recall that
\ell_{k}
is the length of the longest element of
U_{k}
, while
\psi_{k}
is a minimal lift of
\varphi_{k}\circ\pi
relative to
U_{k}
.
By
Proposition 3.14
, we have
\lambda_{\infty}(\psi_{k},U)\geqslant\rho(n_{k})/100\ell_{k}
and the conclusion follows from our choice of
M_{k}
.

It follows from our claim that
\lambda_{\infty}(\psi_{k},U)
diverges to infinity, that is
(G,\pi)
is a divergent limit group.
Proceeding as in
Section 7
, we build an action of
G
on a limit
\mathbf{R}
-tree
T
.
This action decomposes as a graph of action, where each vertex action is either peripheral, simplicial, axial or has Seifert type (
Theorem 7.44
).
We denote by
S
the complete splitting of
G
with respect to
T
, see
Definition 7.45
.
We write
(\hat{G}_{k},\hat{\pi}_{k},\hat{S}_{k})
for a sequence of finitely presented strong covers of
(G,\pi,S)
that converges to
(G,\pi,S)
, see
Definition 5.7
.
For every
k\in\mathbf{N}
, we denote by
\zeta_{k}\colon\hat{G}_{k}\twoheadrightarrow G
the associated projection.
Up to replacing the original sequence
(\varphi_{k})
by a subsequence, we can always assume that
\ker\hat{\pi}_{k}
is generated (as a normal subgroup) by
U_{k}\cap\ker\hat{\pi}_{k}
.
By construction,

U_{k}\cap\ker\hat{\pi}_{k}\subset U_{k}\cap\ker\pi\subset U_{k}\cap\ker(\varphi_{k}\circ\pi)\subset U_{k}\cap\ker\psi_{k},

thus
\ker\hat{\pi}_{k}
lies in the kernel of
\psi_{k}
.
Hence
\psi_{k}\colon F\to\Gamma_{j_{k},k}
factors through
\hat{\pi}_{k}\colon F\twoheadrightarrow\hat{G}_{k}
.
We write
\hat{\psi}_{k}\colon\hat{G}_{k}\to\Gamma_{j_{k},k}
for the resulting morphism.

According to our assumption
G
is freely indecomposable and does not admit any root splitting.
Hence we can perform the shortening argument (
Section 7.5
) with
(\hat{G}_{k},\hat{\pi}_{k},\hat{S}_{k})
as the sequence of strong covers.
By
Theorem 7.46
, there exists
\kappa>0
, as well as automorphisms
\alpha_{k}
in
\operatorname{Mod}(G,S)
and
\hat{\alpha}_{k}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{Aut}\left(\hat{G}_{k}\right)}{\operatorname{Aut}(\hat{G}_{k})}{\operatorname{Aut}(\hat{G}_{k})}{\operatorname{Aut}(\hat{G}_{k})}
such that for infinitely many
k\in\mathbf{N}
, we have
\alpha_{k}\circ\zeta_{k}=\zeta_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}
and

\lambda_{1}^{+}(\hat{\psi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\pi}_{k},U)<(1-\kappa)\lambda_{1}^{+}(\psi_{k},U)

Observe that
\hat{\psi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\pi}_{k}
is an approximation of
\varphi_{k}\circ\alpha_{k}\circ\pi
.
Since
\varphi_{k}\circ\pi
is
\varepsilon
-short, this approximation is necessarily minimal.
Moreover it satisfies

\lambda_{1}^{+}(\psi_{k},U)\leqslant\lambda_{1}^{+}(\hat{\psi}_{k}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{k}\circ\hat{\pi}_{k},U)+\varepsilon\leqslant(1-\kappa)\lambda_{1}^{+}(\psi_{k},U)+\varepsilon.

Recall that
\lambda_{\infty}(\psi_{k},U)
diverges to infinity.
This leads to a contradiction if
k
is sufficiently large, whence the result.
∎

Corollary 8.7
.

Let
G
be a finitely generated, freely indecomposable group with no essential root splitting.
Let
\Gamma
be a non-elementary, torsion-free, hyperbolic group.
There exist a finite subset
W\subset G\setminus\{1\}
and a critical exponent
N\in\mathbf{N}
, with the following properties.
For every odd integer
n\geqslant N
, for every morphism
\varphi\colon G\to\Gamma/\Gamma^{n}
whose image is not abelian, one of the following holds:

—

there is an automorphism
\alpha\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{Aut}\left(G\right)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}
such that
W\cap\ker(\varphi\circ\alpha)\neq\emptyset
,

—

there is a monomorphism
\tilde{\varphi}\colon G\to\Gamma
lifting
\varphi
.

Proof.

Let us first introduce all the necessary objects.
The Cayley graph
X
of
\Gamma
is a
\tau
-bootstrap for some
\tau\in(0,1)
(and for every exponent
n\in\mathbf{N}
).
Let
U
be a finite generating set of
G
and
F
the free group generated by
U
.
Let
\pi\colon F\twoheadrightarrow G
be the corresponding epimorphism.
Recall that torsion-free hyperbolic groups are equationally noetherian.
Hence there exists a finite subset
V\subset\ker\pi
such that for every morphism
\varphi\colon F\to\Gamma
, if
V
is contained in
\ker\varphi
, then
\varphi
factors through
\pi
.
We write
W\subset G\setminus\{1\}
,
\lambda\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
, and
N\in\mathbf{N}
for the objects provided by
Proposition 8.5
.
In this proof the group
\Gamma
and the space
X
are fixed.
Hence, up to conjugacy, there are only finitely morphisms
\psi\colon G\to\Gamma
such that
\lambda_{\infty}(\psi,U)\leqslant\lambda
.
Up to replacing
W
by a larger subset of
G\setminus\{1\}
, we can assume that for every non-injective such morphism
\psi\colon G\to\Gamma
, we have
W\cap\ker\psi\neq\emptyset
.

Let
n\geqslant N
be an odd integer.
Let
\varphi\colon G\to\Gamma/\Gamma^{n}
be a morphism.
Assume that
W\cap\ker(\varphi\circ\alpha)
is empty for every
\alpha\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{Aut}\left(G\right)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}
.
We are going to prove that
\varphi
admits an injective lift.
According to
Proposition 8.5
, there exist
\alpha\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{Aut}\left(G\right)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}{\operatorname{Aut}(G)}
and a morphism
\psi\colon F\to\Gamma
lifting
\varphi\circ\alpha\circ\pi
relative to
V
and such that
\lambda_{\infty}(\psi,U)\leqslant\lambda
.
It follows from our choice of
V
that
\psi
factors through
\pi
.
In particular, the resulting morphism
\psi_{0}\colon G\to\Gamma
lifts
\varphi\circ\alpha
.
Hence
\psi_{0}\circ\alpha^{-1}
lifts
\varphi
.
In order to complete our proof it suffices to show that
\psi_{0}
is one-to-one.
By construction
\lambda_{\infty}(\psi_{0},U)\leqslant\lambda
.
Note that
W\cap\ker\psi_{0}\subset W\cap\ker(\varphi\circ\alpha)
.
According to our assumption,
W\cap\ker\psi_{0}
is empty.
Thanks to the adjustment we made on the set
W
we can conclude that
\psi_{0}
is one-to-one.
∎

Proposition 8.8
.

Let
F
be a finitely generated free group and
U
be a free basis of
F
.
Let
\pi\colon F\twoheadrightarrow G
be an epimorphism onto a freely indecomposable group distinct from
\mathbf{Z}
.
For every
\lambda\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
, there is a finite subset
V\subset F
with the following property.
Let
A
and
B
be two non-trivial groups and
X
the Bass-Serre tree associated to the free product
\Gamma=A\ast B
.
Let
\varphi\colon F\to\Gamma
be a morphism.
If
\lambda_{\infty}(\varphi,U)\leqslant\lambda
(where the energy is measured in
X
) and
V\cap\ker\pi=V\cap\ker\varphi
, then the image of
\varphi
is contained in a conjugate of
A
or
B
.

Proof.

Let
\lambda\in\mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}
.
We fix a non-decreasing exhaustion
(V_{k})
of
F
by finite subsets.
Assume that the statement is false.
For every
k\in\mathbf{N}
, there exist two non trivial groups
A_{k}
and
B_{k}
and a morphism
\varphi_{k}\colon F\to A_{k}\ast B_{k}
such that if
X_{k}
stands for the Bass-Serre tree associated to
\Gamma_{k}=A_{k}\ast B_{k}
, then the following holds

A.

\lambda(\varphi_{k},U)\leqslant\lambda
, (where the energy is measured in
X_{k}
);

B.

V_{k}\cap\ker\pi=V_{k}\cap\ker\varphi_{k}
;

C.

the image of
\varphi_{k}
is not contained in a conjugate of
A_{k}
of
B_{k}
.

Let
(\varepsilon_{k})
be a sequence converging to zero.
For every
k\in\mathbf{N}
, we fix a basepoint
o_{k}\in X_{k}
such that
\lambda_{\infty}(\varphi_{k},U,o_{k})\leqslant\lambda_{\infty}(\varphi_{k},U)+\varepsilon_{k}
.
In addition we choose a non-principal ultra-filter
\omega
.
We denote by
X_{\omega}
the
\omega
-limit of the sequence of pointed space
(X_{k},o_{k})
.
Note that we do not rescale the space
X_{k}
here.
By construction,
X_{k}
is a simplicial tree for every
k\in\mathbf{N}
, hence so it
X_{\omega}
.
For every
k\in\mathbf{N}
, the morphism
\varphi_{k}
defines an action of
F
on
X_{k}
.
Since its energy is uniformly bounded, it induces a simplicial action of
F
on
X_{\omega}
.
We get from
(C2)(T2)B
that
(\Gamma_{k},\varphi_{k})
converges to
(G,\pi)
for the topology of marked groups.
Consequently
\ker\pi
acts trivially on
X_{\omega}
.
In particular, the previous action induces an action of
G
on
X_{\omega}
.
One checks that for every edge
e
of
X_{\omega}
its stabilizer (in
G
) is trivial.
However
G
is freely indecomposable and distinct from
